- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Gajah Mada, leader of Indonesia (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Indonesia
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Kris Swordsman, Replacing Swordsman (Requires technology: Iron Working)
        - Unique Unit: Djong, Replacing Galleass (Requires technology: Guilds)
        - Unique Building: Candi, Replacing Garden (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Unique Improvement: Kampong (Unique Indonesian Improvement. The Kampong can be built by Embarked Workers on Coastal and Lake Tiles, and not next to another Kampong.
          Each Kampong yields Food, Production, and Culture.); Requires technology: Sailing
        - Civilization Ability: When you gain a City, one of 3 unique Luxuries (//) will appear nearby. +5% to unique Yield and Golden Age duration modifiers from Global Monopolies; +2 to Yields and Happiness from Global Monopolies.
      - Leader: Gajah Mada
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      
      ## Policies
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Militarism (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Culture in Cities with at least 10 Strength.
        - * -15% Unit Maintenance.
        - * -50% Route Maintenance.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Rationalism (New Branch):
        - Rationalism focuses on maximizing Growth and Science output.
        - Adopting Rationalism grants:
        - * +2 Production and +3 Science from all Strategic Resources.
        - * +5% Science in all Cities.
        - Each Rationalism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +2% Science.
        - Adopting all Policies in Rationalism grants:
        - * Unlocks building Bletchley Park.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +12 City Security in every City.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Scientists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        - Rationale: Persona settings were previously adjusted for the Brazil war situation and remain appropriate. The defensive posture, high boldness, and diplomatic insurance through Japan are all functioning as intended.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Denmark
        - Public: -40
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Denmark is an active war enemy. Their DoF partner Brazil is about to fall, and we need to track this relationship correctly as we prepare for potential peace or continued conflict.
        - UpdatedTurn: 301
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: -10
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Japan is actively waging war against us. Their military is stronger but we're holding our positions. Need to maintain hostile stance as we prepare for potential future offensive operations.
        - UpdatedTurn: 344
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: 15
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Brazil is our loyal vassal. Strengthening this relationship ensures their loyalty and prevents any internal instability that could undermine our diplomatic position at the World Congress.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: 20
        - Private: 25
        - Rationale: The Iroquois is our key World Congress ally. Strengthening relations ensures we can successfully block Japan's Sanction: Brazil proposal and pass our combined initiatives.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Current strategy remains optimal. Atomic Theory completion provides nuclear capability to counter Japan's conventional superiority (13016 vs 7148). The strategy of defensive military posture while leveraging WC alliance with The Iroquois (41 combined votes) continues to serve our interests. No immediate changes needed - focus on completing Rocketry (10 turns) to fully leverage nuclear capabilities. The recent loss of Anti-Aircraft Guns and a Destroyer to Japan are manageable losses that don't warrant strategic pivot.

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Open Door: Malacca:

          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: 388
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 366

              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 388
          - Sanction: Japan:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 377
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Iroquois Sphere of Influence: Bunkeya:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 366
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Iroquois Sphere of Influence: Vancouver:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Shinto:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Repeal: Open Door: Segou:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - Proposer: Indonesia
          - Enact: Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - Proposer: Japan
          - Enact: World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - Proposer: The Iroquois

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Gajah Mada, leader of Indonesia (Opponent 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Indonesia
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Kris Swordsman, Replacing Swordsman (Requires technology: Iron Working)
        - Unique Unit: Djong, Replacing Galleass (Requires technology: Guilds)
        - Unique Building: Candi, Replacing Garden (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Unique Improvement: Kampong (Unique Indonesian Improvement. The Kampong can be built by Embarked Workers on Coastal and Lake Tiles, and not next to another Kampong.
          Each Kampong yields Food, Production, and Culture.); Requires technology: Sailing
        - Civilization Ability: When you gain a City, one of 3 unique Luxuries (//) will appear nearby. +5% to unique Yield and Golden Age duration modifiers from Global Monopolies; +2 to Yields and Happiness from Global Monopolies.
      - Leader: Gajah Mada
      - OpponentID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the world (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      
      ## Policies
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Militarism (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Culture in Cities with at least 10 Strength.
        - * -15% Unit Maintenance.
        - * -50% Route Maintenance.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Rationalism (New Branch):
        - Rationalism focuses on maximizing Growth and Science output.
        - Adopting Rationalism grants:
        - * +2 Production and +3 Science from all Strategic Resources.
        - * +5% Science in all Cities.
        - Each Rationalism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +2% Science.
        - Adopting all Policies in Rationalism grants:
        - * Unlocks building Bletchley Park.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +12 City Security in every City.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Scientists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Nuclear Fission
        - Rationale: Medan is completing the Manhattan Project in 3 turns. Researching Nuclear Fission next will allow us to build Atomic Bombs using our newly secured Uranium resources, providing the ultimate counteroffensive capability against Japan's massive conventional army.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Selecting the Patriotic War Order tenet will give our land units +20% combat strength when adjacent to or inside our cities, which is critical for defending our front line against Japan's superior military forces.
        - Policy: Patriotic War
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - Flavors:
          - Defense: 80
          - Nuke: 90
          - Espionage: 70
          - Diplomacy: 50
          - Science: 80
          - Gold: 60
          - Mobilization: 80
          - Production: 70
          - UseNuke: 80
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
      - rationale: Adjusting flavors to prioritize wartime efficiency, fast-tracking science for Nuclear Fission, mobilizing production for military units and nuclear weapons, and increasing diplomacy to support the critical upcoming World Congress vote.
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Rationale: Egypt is our Defensive Pact ally and is actively fighting Japan. Solidifying our relationship is crucial for mutual defense and cooperation.
        - Private: 40
        - TargetID: 0
        - Public: 40
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: 30
        - TargetID: 1
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Brazil is our loyal vassal and is currently under attack by Japan. Strengthening relations ensures their continued support and stability.
    - #5:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 40
        - Rationale: The Iroquois is our critical World Congress ally. Strengthening our relationship is vital to pass World Ideology: Order and block Japan's Passport System proposal.
        - TargetID: 2
    - #6:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 50
        - Rationale: Denmark is our close Defensive Pact and DoF ally. Correcting the outdated hostile modifiers from Turn 301 to reflect our strong friendship and alliance.
        - TargetID: 3
    - #7:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 7
        - Rationale: Japan is our existential threat, close to a cultural victory, and currently at war with us. We must set our relationship to maximum hostility to reflect this total war.
        - Private: -100
        - Public: -100
  - tokens:

    - reasoningTokens: 6810
    - outputTokens: 473
  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Our Dominance**
            
            My focus is on solidifying our position. With 13 cities and a population of 307, I'm confident we're on track to secure our victory, currently sitting comfortably in second place as the Atomic Era progresses.
            
            **Assessing Competitive Landscape**
            
            I'm currently evaluating Japan's strong cultural push. They've influenced all other civilizations, positioning them as a significant threat to a cultural victory. My current focus is on understanding the precise mechanics of their win condition and identifying countermeasures.
            
            **Countering Cultural Victory**
            
            I'm analyzing Japan's advanced position in the Information Era, noting their 67 technologies compared to our 62. My immediate concern is their imminent cultural victory, potentially accelerated by the Passport System proposal in the World Congress. Simultaneously, I'm tracking our current wars against them, assessing their impact.
            
            **Analyzing Military Imbalance**
            
            I'm assessing our current military deficit against Japan and the Iroquois, acknowledging our significant gap. My immediate priority is to advance our research toward Rocketry, aiming to bridge this disparity and explore potential policy shifts, particularly within the Order branch, to bolster our defenses and strategic options.
            
            **Revising Ideological Strategy**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our Order policies given our low gold income and the imminent Manhattan Project completion. I need to confirm if we can still adopt new tenets within Order or if prioritizing a new branch like Rationalism or Industry is a better path, especially considering our ongoing wars and military disparity.
            
            **Researching Critical Technologies**
            
            I'm prioritizing Nuclear Fission, realizing it's available as a research option. This is crucial for unlocking Atomic Bombs, especially with the Manhattan Project nearing completion and our existing Uranium. I need to confirm the exact research time and if any immediate policy changes are needed.
            
            **Prioritizing Atomic Advancement**
            
            I'm focusing on acquiring Nuclear Fission next. This aligns perfectly with the nearing completion of the Manhattan Project, and ensures we'll have the Uranium needed for Atomic Bombs to counter Japan's aggression.
            
            **Adopting Defensive Policies**
            
            I'm prioritizing the "Patriotic War" tenet within Order. Given our defensive stance against Japan and the proximity of their armies to our front-line cities, the +20% strength bonus in and around our cities is critically important for survival.
            
            **Choosing Defensive Policies**
            
            I've decided to adopt the "Patriotic War" tenet. While the free Factories are less impactful given my existing infrastructure, the T-34 tanks and the crucial +20% combat strength bonus near my cities are vital for countering Japan's offensive and ensuring my empire's survival.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Alignments**
            
            I'm assessing our allies' stances. Egypt is actively fighting Japan, while Denmark and the Iroquois have recently made peace. My current focus is understanding why some allies aren't joining our war with Japan and preparing for the upcoming World Congress vote.
            
            **Opposing Destructive Policies**
            
            I'm focused on stopping the Passport System proposed by Japan. Its tourism boost is a direct threat to my progress, especially with Japan so close to a cultural victory. I'm evaluating how to best use my diplomatic influence to ensure this proposal fails.
            
            **Securing Congressional Dominance**
            
            I've realized that with five out of six civilizations adhering to the Order ideology, a vote for "World Ideology: Order" would grant us significant delegate advantages, effectively neutralizing Japan and allowing us to dominate the World Congress. I need to ensure we vote yes on this and no on the Passport System.
            
            **Securing Ideological Victory**
            
            I'm focusing on leveraging our dominant Order alignment to push through favorable World Congress resolutions. My priority is ensuring "World Ideology: Order" passes to neutralize Japan's influence, while simultaneously blocking their dangerous Passport System. This will solidify our diplomatic standing and hinder their cultural victory path.
            
            **Accelerating Atomic Bomb Production**
            
            I'm shifting focus to accelerating science to rush Rocketry and then Nuclear Fission. This is critical to obtain Atomic Bombs and decimate Japan's large ground forces, particularly their massive stack in Brasilia. My aim is to then push into Kyoto and secure victory.
            
            **Adjusting Flavor Allocation**
            
            I'm reallocating our flavor points. Specifically, I'm boosting Science and Mobilization to accelerate research and military production. Diplomacy and Gold are also receiving attention to enhance our World Congress influence and unit maintenance capabilities, while maintaining high Espionage for intelligence gathering against Japan.
            
            **Correcting Diplomatic Errors**
            
            I've discovered a critical oversight: Denmark, a defensive pact ally, was erroneously marked as an enemy with negative relationship modifiers from a past era. I am correcting these outdated values to accurately reflect our current strong alliance, ensuring our diplomatic front remains solid.
            
            **Strengthening Diplomatic Ties**
            
            I'm rectifying past diplomatic oversights by ensuring all allies and vassals have appropriately friendly relationship modifiers. This includes setting Denmark, Iroquois, Brazil, and Egypt to strong positive values to solidify our alliances and ensure continued support, especially in the World Congress.
            
            **Refining Diplomatic Strategy**
            
            I'm now precisely configuring the relationship modifiers for all key civilizations. My focus is on ensuring Japan is set to maximum hostility, while strengthening ties with Denmark, Egypt, and the Iroquois to secure crucial World Congress votes.
            
            **Solidifying Alliance Bonds**
            
            I'm ensuring all relationships are meticulously set. Japan is marked for maximum hostility, while my allies like Denmark, Egypt, and the Iroquois are receiving strengthened ties to secure vital World Congress support. This strategic diplomatic realignment is paramount.
            
            **Optimizing Resource Allocation**
            
            My current focus is on fine-tuning our flavor settings to maximize scientific advancement and military output. I'm allocating 80 points to Science to expedite research towards Rocketry and Nuclear Fission, and 70 to Production for rapid Atomic Bomb and unit construction, while also prioritizing Nuke at 90.
            
            **Adjusting Resource Priorities**
            
            My current focus is on optimizing flavor allocation. I'm prioritizing Science and Production, aiming to accelerate research towards Nuclear Fission and subsequently increase our Atomic Bomb and unit construction speed. I've also boosted Nuke usage and Espionage to decisively counter Japan's aggression.
            
            **Prioritizing Nuclear Advancements**
            
            I'm now prioritizing Nuclear Fission research, aligning perfectly with the imminent completion of the Manhattan Project. This ensures we can immediately leverage our Uranium for Atomic Bombs to counter Japan's overwhelming military presence and secure a decisive victory.
            
            **Adjusting Focus for Victory**
            
            I'm now reallocating my priorities to expedite research towards Nuclear Fission and further boost production for Atomic Bombs and military units. I'm also ensuring my diplomatic efforts are aligned to secure crucial World Congress votes, specifically targeting "World Ideology: Order" and blocking Japan's Passport System.
            
            **Finalizing Diplomatic Configurations**
            
            I'm completing the meticulous configuration of all civilization relationship modifiers. Japan is set to maximum hostility, while Denmark, Egypt, and the Iroquois are receiving enhanced ties to secure critical World Congress votes. This strategic diplomatic refinement ensures our strategic goals align.
